        </ul>
      </div>
      <div class="main-nav__mobile-toggles">
        <div class="main-nav__s-icon-mobile">
          <a href="javascript:;" class="open-search-popup"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="" data-lazy-src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/search-icon.svg"><noscript><img src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/search-icon.svg" alt=""></noscript></a>
          <a href="javascript:;" class="close-search-popup hidden"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="" data-lazy-src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/close-24.svg"><noscript><img src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/close-24.svg" alt=""></noscript></a>
        </div>
        <a class="main-nav__mobile-menu"></a>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>
</div>

<div data-rocket-location-hash="69c3e2a4c1b30ea1d8ad0439e0b2cf8f" id="popup" class="search-popup">
  <div data-rocket-location-hash="25109af7ba5bbce12aeeac7bb1cba281" class="popup-content">

  .hidden {
    display: none;

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
  const openSearchPopupBtns = [...document.querySelectorAll('.open-search-popup')];
  const closeSearchPopupBtns = [...document.querySelectorAll('.close-search-popup')];
  const searchPopup = document.querySelector('.search-popup');

  const closePopup = () => {
    searchPopup.style.display = 'none';
    openSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.remove('hidden'));
    closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.add('hidden'));
  };

  openSearchPopupBtns.forEach(openSearchPopupBtn => {
    openSearchPopupBtn.addEventListener('click', (e) => {
      e.preventDefault();

      searchPopup.style.display = 'block';
      openSearchPopupBtn.classList.add('hidden');
      closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.remove('hidden'));
    });
  });

  closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(closeSearchPopupBtn => {
    closeSearchPopupBtn.addEventListener('click', () => {
      closePopup();
    });
  });

  document.addEventListener('click', (event) => {
    if (!searchPopup.contains(event.target) && !openSearchPopupBtns.some(btn => btn.contains(event.target)) && !closeSearchPopupBtns.some(btn => btn.contains(event.target))) {
      setTimeout(() => {
        if (!searchPopup.contains(event.target)) {
          closePopup();
        }
      }, 100);
    }
  });
</script>    </div>

                            <h1 class="header__title title-h2 mb-24 mt-md-48 mt-lg-64 mt-xl-64">The footprints of Raccoon</h1><p class="header__text mb-xs-48 mb-64 mb-lg-96 mb-xl-96">A story about operators of JS-sniffer FakeSecurity distributing Raccoon stealer</p><div class="post-info__date mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48"><div class="body2">December 7, 2020 · <span id="reading-time"></span> min to read · Threat Intelligence</div></div><div class="post-info__image mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/img-1440-6.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="" class="webpexpress-processed" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/img-1440-6.jpg"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/img-1440-6.jpg" alt="" class="webpexpress-processed"></noscript></picture></div><div class="post-info__tags"><div class="post-info__tag body2">JS sniffers</div><div class="post-info__tag body2">Stealers</div><div class="post-info__tag body2">Threat Intelligence</div></div>                        </div>
                    </div>
                </div>
                <div class="blog-post-content" id="blog-post-content">
                    <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><h2>Introduction</h2>
<p>In the summer of 2020, Group-IB specialists discovered a malware distribution campaign exploiting Telegram&#8217;s legitimate features. Analysis showed that the attackers used the technique to distribute Raccoon stealer, i.e. malware spread through the Malware-as-a-Service model on one of darknet forums. They, in particular, used Telegram channels in order to bypass blocking of active C&amp;C servers.</p>
<p>Raccoon Stealer collects system information, account data, bank card data, and autofill form details from browsers (Google Chrome, Mozilla Firefox, Opera, etc.). What&#8217;s more, Raccoon Stealer scans the infected device for information about valid crypto wallets. If successful, it gains access to configuration files.</p>
<div id="attachment_26563" style="width: 1690px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-21.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26563" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26563 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201680%20854'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Ad of Raccoon stealer on one of underground forums" width="1680" height="854" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-21.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26563" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26563 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-21.png" alt="Ad of Raccoon stealer on one of underground forums" width="1680" height="854"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26563" class="wp-caption-text">Ad of Raccoon stealer on one of underground forums (translation is provided below)</p></div>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="accordionList433"><div class="accordion-item"><div class="accordion-item__title"><div class="title-h5">Translation</div><div class="accordion-item__icon"><span class="material-icons">arrow_drop_down</span></div></div><div class="accordion-item__text"><p><strong>Raccoon Stealer. We steal, You deal!</strong></p>
<p>Our team proudly presents the result of many months of work.<br />
Stealing logs has never been so easy and straightforward and sorting them as never been so fast and comfortable. We deal with all the frustrating, time-consuming, and tedious issues so that you can focus on what&#8217;s important: increasing your revenue.</p>
<p>Forget about routing and maintaining servers, assembling builds, and other problems. We&#8217;ve gone automatic — all you need is a few clicks.</p>
<p>Our specialists work in three areas: software, front-end, and back-end. It helps us focus on specific goals and release a complete product.</p>
<p>New software:</p>
<ul>
<li>Exclusive code. Unique build</li>
<li>C/C++ stealer with enhanced performance</li>
<li>Excellent signal for each entry; only some antivirus software detects Raccoon during dynamic testing</li>
<li>Raccoon collects passwords, cookies, autofill data from all popular browsers (including FireFox x64), CC data, system information, and almost all types of desktop crypto wallets</li>
<li>Embedded downloader</li>
<li>Compatible with x32 and x64 operating systems regardless of .NET</li>
<li>You get an easy-to-encrypt Native x86 executable file</li>
<li>Private key, gate address, and other string values are heavily encrypted</li>
</ul>
</div></div>
                      <script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
                        jQuery(window).on('load', function () {
                          let accordion433 = document.querySelector('.accordionList433');
                          if (accordion433) {
                            let items = accordion433.querySelectorAll('.accordion-item');
                            let title = accordion433.querySelectorAll('.accordion-item__title');

                            function toggleAccordion() {
                              let thisItem = this.parentNode;
                              items.forEach((item) => {
                                if (thisItem == item) {
                                  thisItem.classList.toggle('active');
                                  return;
                                }
                                item.classList.remove('active');
                              });
                            }

                            title.forEach((question) => question.addEventListener('click', toggleAccordion));
                          } 
                        });
                      </script>
                      </div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>During research, Group-IB <a href="/products/threat-intelligence/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Threat Intelligence</a> experts established links with other elements of the threat actors&#8217; infrastructure and recreated the malicious campaign timeline. The campaign was divided into four stages based on the tools used (type of malware, registrars for creating infrastructure, etc.):</p>
<ul>
<li>First wave: February 19 to March 5 2020</li>
<li>Second wave: March 13 to May 22, 2020</li>
<li>Third wave: June 29 to July 2, 2020</li>
<li>Fourth wave: August 24 to September 12, 2020</li>
</ul>
<p>Most domains related to the investigated campaign were registered with two registrars: <strong>Cloud2m</strong> and <strong>Host Africa</strong>. Cloud2m was used in earlier attacks. In mid-July 2020, some of these domains moved to Host Africa.</p>
<div id="attachment_26564" style="width: 1210px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-27.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26564" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26564 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201200%20675'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Timeline of FakeSecurity's malicious campaign infographic" width="1200" height="675" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-27.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26564" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26564 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-27.png" alt="Timeline of FakeSecurity's malicious campaign infographic" width="1200" height="675"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26564" class="wp-caption-text">Timeline of FakeSecurity&#8217;s malicious campaign from February to September 2020</p></div>
<p>Group-IB experts concluded that the purpose of the campaign in question was to steal payment and user data. The attackers used several attack vectors and tools to deliver the malware.</p>
<p>It was also discovered that in early 2020, before distributing the Raccoon stealer, the attackers had distributed samples of another stealer called Vidar. To do so, they used attachments with malicious macros and phishing pages created with the Mephistophilus phishing kit.</p>
<div id="attachment_26565" style="width: 1210px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/3-min-28.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26565" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26565 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201200%20675'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Infection pattern in the malicious campaigns of hacker group FakeSecurity" width="1200" height="675" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/3-min-28.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26565" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26565 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/3-min-28.png" alt="Infection pattern in the malicious campaigns of hacker group FakeSecurity" width="1200" height="675"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26565" class="wp-caption-text">Infection pattern in the malicious campaigns of hacker group FakeSecurity</p></div>
<p>This malware distribution technique reminded Group-IB experts of the pattern used by FakeSecurity JS-sniffer operators during the campaign <a href="https://www.group-ib.com/blog/fakesecurity/">described</a> in November 2019. Apart from having similar toolkits, both series of attacks targeted e-commerce. In May 2020, Group-IB identified online stores that had been infected with a modified JS-sniffer of the FakeSecurity family. The JS-sniffer was obfuscated using the <strong>aaencode</strong> algorithm, while the domains used to store the code and collect stolen bank card data were registered during the second wave with the same registrars as the domains that we discovered while investigating the malicious campaign. As such, it can be assumed that FakeSecurity JS-sniffer operators were behind the stealer distribution campaign.</p>
<div id="attachment_26566" style="width: 1510px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/4-min-28.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26566" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26566 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201500%201745'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Domain infrastructure of FakeSecurity's malicious campaigns" width="1500" height="1745" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/4-min-28.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26566" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26566 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/4-min-28.png" alt="Domain infrastructure of FakeSecurity's malicious campaigns" width="1500" height="1745"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26566" class="wp-caption-text">Domain infrastructure of FakeSecurity&#8217;s malicious campaigns</p></div>
<h2>First wave</h2>
<p>The first wave of domain registrations began in the <strong>co.za</strong> zone on February 19, 2020. The suspicious domains contained the following keywords: <strong>cloud, document</strong>, and <strong>Microsoft</strong>. Examples of domains registered during the first wave are presented below:</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="table"><div class="table__wrapper"><table width="100%" data-table-width="25%;20%;15%;15%;25%">
<tbody>
<tr>
<td width="25%">msupdater[.]co.za</td>
<td width="20%">2020-02-19</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">documents-cloud-server[.]co.za</td>
<td width="20%">2020-03-05</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">cloudupdate[.]co.za</td>
<td width="20%">2020-02-21</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>As part of the campaign&#8217;s first wave, the initial compromise vector used: (i) mailings with attachments containing malicious macros and (ii) phishing pages leading to malware downloading.</p>
<h2>Documents with macros</h2>
<p>On February 28, nine days after the first domain was registered, the file &#8220;<em>Bank001.xlsm</em>&#8221; (SHA1: b1799345152f0f11a0a573b91093a1867d64e119) was uploaded to VirusTotal via a US web interface.</p>
<div id="attachment_26567" style="width: 1693px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min-30.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26567" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26567 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201683%20705'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="lure document screenshot" width="1683" height="705" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min-30.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26567" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26567 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min-30.png" alt="lure document screenshot" width="1683" height="705"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26567" class="wp-caption-text">SHA1: b1799345152f0f11a0a<br />573b91093a1867d64e119 lure document. Alert says: &#8220;SECURITY WARNING. Macros have been disabled. &#8220;Enable content.&#8221;&#8221;</p></div>
<p>The file is a lure document with malicious macros. When activated, it downloads a payload from <em>http://cloudupdate.co[.]za/documents/msofficeupdate.exe.</em></p>
<div id="attachment_26568" style="width: 1338px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/6-min-22.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26568" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26568 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201328%20757'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Malicious macros contained in raccoon stealer lure document" width="1328" height="757" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/6-min-22.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26568" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26568 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/6-min-22.png" alt="Malicious macros contained in raccoon stealer lure document" width="1328" height="757"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26568" class="wp-caption-text">Malicious macros contained in lure document and partially obfuscated in Base64</p></div>
<p>As a result, the file &#8220;<strong>msofficeupdate.exe</strong>&#8221; (SHA1: f3498ba783b9c8c84d754af8a687d2ff189615d9) is executed. The C&amp;C server in this case is badlandsparks[.]com. This domain was registered on February 27, 2020 and is associated with the IP address 185.244.149[.]100. More than 30 files connect to this domain alone.</p>
<p><div id="attachment_26569" style="width: 1849px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/7-min-24.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26569" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26569 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201839%20980'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Infrastructure relating to the raccoon stealer domain" width="1839" height="980" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/7-min-24.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26569" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26569 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/7-min-24.png" alt="Infrastructure relating to the raccoon stealer domain" width="1839" height="980"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26569" class="wp-caption-text">Infrastructure relating to the domain badlandsparks[.]com established with the help of Group-IB Graph Network Analysis</p></div>These files include &#8220;<em>13b7afe8ee87977ae34734812482ca7efd62b9b6</em>&#8221; and &#8220;<em>596a3cb4d82e6ab4d7223be640f614b0f7bd14af</em>&#8220;. They create a network connection to <em>gineuter[.]info, fastandprettycleaner[.]hk</em> and <em>badlandsparks[.]com</em>. Judging by the requests they make to download libraries and open source data, the file &#8220;<em>msofficeupdate.exe</em>&#8221; and others like it are samples of the Vidar stealer. Criminals use the stealer to collect data from browsers (including web browsing history and account data), bank card data, crypto wallet files, messages, and more.</p>
<div id="attachment_26570" style="width: 1689px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/8-min-23.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26570" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26570 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201679%20799'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Vidar stealer admin panel" width="1679" height="799" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/8-min-23.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26570" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26570 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/8-min-23.png" alt="Vidar stealer admin panel" width="1679" height="799"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26570" class="wp-caption-text">Vidar stealer admin panel</p></div>
<div id="attachment_26571" style="width: 1849px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/9-min-17.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26571" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26571 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201839%20821'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="raccoon stealer network communication" width="1839" height="821" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/9-min-17.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26571" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26571 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/9-min-17.png" alt="raccoon stealer network communication" width="1839" height="821"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26571" class="wp-caption-text">SHA1: 596a3cb4d82e6ab4d7223be640f614b0f7bd14af file network communication built with the help of Group-IB Graph Network Analysis</p></div>
<p>A list of Vidar-specific HTTP requests and a detailed overview are available <a href="https://fumik0.com/2018/12/24/lets-dig-into-vidar-an-arkei-copycat-forked-stealer-in-depth-analysis/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener">here</a>:</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="code"><div class="code__wrapper">/ (i.e 162)    <- Config
ip-api.com/line/    <- Get Network Info
/msvcp140.dll       <- Required DLL
/nss3.dll           <- Required DLL
/softokn3.dll       <- Required DLL
/vcruntime140.dll   <- Required DLL
/                   <- Pushing Victim Archive to C2 
</div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>The file &#8220;<em>BankStatement1.xlsm</em>&#8221; (SHA1: c2f8d217877b1a28e4951286d3375212f8dc2335) is another lure document with malicious macros. When activated, it downloads the file from <em>http://download-plugin[.]co.za/documents/msofficeupdate.exe</em>.</p>
<p>The download file SHA1: 430a406f2134b48908363e473dd6da11a172a7e1 is also a Vidar stealer. The file is available for download here:</p>
<ul>
<li>http://download-plugin.co[.]za/documents/msofficeupdate.exe</li>
<li>http://msupdater.co[.]za/documents/msofficeupdate.exe</li>
<li>http://cloudupdate.co[.]za/documents/msofficeupdate.exe</li>
</ul>
<div id="attachment_26572" style="width: 1287px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/10-min-20.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26572" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26572 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201277%20772'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="example of raccoon stealer malicious file" width="1277" height="772" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/10-min-20.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26572" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26572 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/10-min-20.png" alt="example of raccoon stealer malicious file" width="1277" height="772"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26572" class="wp-caption-text">Example of 430a406f2134b48908363e473dd6da11a172a7e1 file availability from different sources</p></div>
<h2>Mephistophilus phishing kit</h2>
<p>The second attack vector during the first wave was the use of phishing pages to distribute malware.</p>
<p>It turned out that the discovered domains (msupdater[.]co.za, cloudupdate[.]co.za and documents-cloud-server[.]co.za) had the same A record created at the same time: 160.119.253[.]53. According to Group-IB&#8217;s Graph Network Analysis, documents-cloud-server[.]co.za contained the Mephistophilus phishing kit.</p>
<div id="attachment_26530" style="width: 1630px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/11-min-16.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26530" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26530 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201620%20936'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Links between raccoon stealer domains under review" width="1620" height="936" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/11-min-16.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26530" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26530 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/11-min-16.png" alt="Links between raccoon stealer domains under review" width="1620" height="936"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26530" class="wp-caption-text">Links between domains under review established with the help of Group-IB Graph Network Analysis</p></div>
<p>From the start, Mephistophilus has been presented as a system for targeted phishing attacks. This phishing kit contains several fake web page templates for delivering payload, including:</p>
<ul>
<li>Microsoft Office 365, Word, and Excel online viewers</li>
<li>PDF online viewer</li>
<li>YouTube phishing page</li>
</ul>
<div id="attachment_26531" style="width: 744px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/12-min-16.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26531" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26531 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20734%20491'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Mephistophilus admin panel" width="734" height="491" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/12-min-16.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26531" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26531 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/12-min-16.png" alt="Mephistophilus admin panel" width="734" height="491"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26531" class="wp-caption-text">Mephistophilus admin panel</p></div>
<div id="attachment_26532" style="width: 1610px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/13-min-12.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26532" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26532 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201600%201200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Fake Adobe Reader update window" width="1600" height="1200" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/13-min-12.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26532" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26532 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/13-min-12.png" alt="Fake Adobe Reader update window" width="1600" height="1200"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26532" class="wp-caption-text">Fake Adobe Reader update window</p></div>
<p>The <em>documents-cloud-server[.]co.za</em> domain contains a web fake imitating an Adobe Reader plugin update page. To continue viewing the document, the user is asked to download a plugin. By clicking on &#8220;<em>Download plugin</em>,&#8221; the user activates a malware download from <em>http://www.documents-cloud-server[.]co.za/file_d/adobe-reader-update-10.21.01.exe</em>. Source code of phishing content is available here.</p>
<p>A file with the same name &#8220;<em>adobe-reader-update-10.21.01.exe</em>&#8221; (SHA1: f33c1f0930231fe6f5d0f00978188857cbb0e90d) was first uploaded to VirusTotal on March 13, 2020. It was available for download here:</p>
<ul>
<li>http://documents-cloud-server5[.]co.za/file_d/adobe-reader-update-10.21.01.exe</li>
<li>http://documents-cloud-server1[.]co.za/file_d/adobe-reader-update-10.21.01.exe</li>
<li>http://www.documents-cloud-server9[.]co.za/file_d/adobe-reader-update-10.21.01.exe</li>
<li>http://documents-cloud-server8[.]co.za/file_d/adobe-reader-update-10.21.01.exe</li>
</ul>
<div id="attachment_26533" style="width: 1294px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/14-min-15.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26533" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26533 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201284%20771'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="example of raccoon stealer malicious file" width="1284" height="771" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/14-min-15.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26533" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26533 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/14-min-15.png" alt="example of raccoon stealer malicious file" width="1284" height="771"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26533" class="wp-caption-text">Example of f33c1f0930231fe6f5d0f00978188857cbb0e90d file availability from different sources</p></div>
<p>Another file named &#8220;<em>msofficeupdater.exe</em>&#8221; (SHA1: bdfefdff7b755a89d60de22309da72b82df70ecb) was available for download here:</p>
<ul>
<li>http://www.documents-cloud-server7[.]co.za/doc/msofficeupdater.exe</li>
<li>http://documents-cloud-server5[.]co.za/doc/msofficeupdater.exe</li>
<li>http://documents-cloud-server7[.]co.za/doc/msofficeupdater.exe</li>
<li>http://www.documents-cloud-server6[.]co.za/doc/msofficeupdater.exe</li>
<li>http://documents-cloud-server1[.]co.za/doc/msofficeupdater.exe</li>
<li>http://documents-cloud-server6[.]co.za/doc/msofficeupdater.exe</li>
<li>http://www.documents-cloud-server5[.]co.za/doc/msofficeupdater.exe</li>
<li>http://www.documents-cloud-server1[.]co.za/doc/msofficeupdater.exe</li>
</ul>
<div id="attachment_26534" style="width: 1310px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/15-min-15.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26534" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26534 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201300%20856'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="example of raccoon stealer malicious file" width="1300" height="856" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/15-min-15.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26534" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26534 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/15-min-15.png" alt="example of raccoon stealer malicious file" width="1300" height="856"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26534" class="wp-caption-text">Example of bdfefdff7b755a89d60de22309da72b82df70ecb file availability from different sources</p></div>
<h2>Second wave</h2>
<p>The domains associated with the file SHA1: bdfefdff7b755a89d60de22309da72b82df70ecb led us to another batch of domains related to the attackers&#8217; infrastructure. The domains were registered in two stages: the first batch on March 13, 2020 and the second one on May 22, 2020. Examples of second-wave domains:</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="table"><div class="table__wrapper"><table width="100%" data-table-width="25%;20%;15%;15%;25%">
<thead>
<tr>
<th width="25%">Batch 1</th>
<th width="20%">Batch 2</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td width="25%">cloud-server-updater[.]co.za</td>
<td width="20%">cloud-server-updater17[.]co.za</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">cloud-server-updater1[.]co.za</td>
<td width="20%">cloud-server-updater18[.]co.za</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">cloud-server-updater15[.]co.za</td>
<td width="20%">cloud-server-updater27[.]co.za</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="25%">cloud-server-updater16[.]co.za</td>
<td width="20%">cloud-server-updater28[.]co.za</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>These domains were created to distribute the Raccoon stealer. It is possible to establish the connection between these domain batches by looking at SHA1: b326f9a6d6087f10ef3a9f554a874243f000549d and SHA1: F2B2F74F4572BF8BD2D948B34147FFE303F92A0F files. When executed, these files establish a network connection to:</p>
<ul>
<li>cloudupdates[.]co.za</li>
<li>cloud-server-updater2[.]co.za</li>
<li>cloud-server-updater19.co.za</li>
</ul>
<div id="attachment_26535" style="width: 1818px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/16-min-14.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26535" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26535 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201808%20817'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="raccoon stealer malicious file network communication" width="1808" height="817" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/16-min-14.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26535" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26535 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/16-min-14.png" alt="raccoon stealer malicious file network communication" width="1808" height="817"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26535" class="wp-caption-text">b326f9a6d6087f10ef3a9f554a874243f000549d file network communication established with the help of Group-IB Graph Network Analysis</p></div>
<p>About 50 malicious files from public sources are related to the domain <em>cloudupdates[.]co.za</em>. Their first uploads date back to April 30, 2020 and the domain is similar to the previously discovered cloudupdate.co[.]za. Besides having a similar domain name, it was registered through the cloud2m registrar and <em>ns1.host-ww.net, ns2.host-ww.net</em> as well as <em>msupdater[.]co.za</em> and <em>cloudupdate[.]co.za</em></p>
<div id="attachment_26536" style="width: 753px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/17-min-10.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26536" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26536 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20743%20173'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="raccoon stealer WHOIS records data from three domains" width="743" height="173" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/17-min-10.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26536" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26536 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/17-min-10.png" alt="raccoon stealer WHOIS records data from three domains" width="743" height="173"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26536" class="wp-caption-text">WHOIS records data from three domains</p></div>
<p>About 300 files from public sandboxes are associated with all the second-wave domains. All these files are lure documents containing malicious macros named &#8220;<em>MyBankStatement_2436.xlsm</em>&#8220;, <em>MyBankStatement_3269.xlsm</em>, &#8220;<em>MyBankStatement_5763.xlsm</em>&#8220;, etc.</p>
<div id="attachment_26537" style="width: 1073px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/18-min-10.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26537" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26537 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201063%20627'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="lure document sample - raccoon stealer" width="1063" height="627" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/18-min-10.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26537" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26537 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/18-min-10.png" alt="lure document sample - raccoon stealer" width="1063" height="627"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26537" class="wp-caption-text">6685955C5F006C2D83A92952EB5EB3FB9598C783 lure document sample</p></div>
<p>One of these files is &#8220;MyBank_5710.xlsm (SHA1: 685955C5F006C2D83A92952EB5EB3FB9598C783). After activating the macros in this document, a file was downloaded from http://cloud-server-updater22[.]co.za/doc/officebuilder. This file with SHA1: 3657CF5F2142C7E30F72E231E87518B82710DC1C is a Raccoon stealer. It connects to the C&amp;C server (35.228.95[.]80) to exfiltrate the collected information, using Google&#8217;s infrastructure to legitimize requests. In turn, Raccoon makes a network connection to http://cloud-server-updater1[.]co.za/doc/officeupdate.exe and downloads RAT AveMaria (SHA1: a10925364347bde843a1d4105dddf4a4eb88c746), with the C&amp;C server located at the IP address 102.130.118[.]152.</p>
<p>AveMaria is a RAT, which was discovered by cybersecurity researchers in late 2018, when it was used to attack an Italian oil and gas company. The RAT is capable of:</p>
<ul>
<li>Privilege escalation</li>
<li>Ensuring persistence in the infected system</li>
<li>Injecting code</li>
<li>Keylogging</li>
<li>Gaining access to web camera</li>
<li>Managing processes</li>
<li>Managing files (creation, download, exfiltration, deletion)</li>
<li>RDP using rdpwrap</li>
<li>Info-stealer support:
<ul>
<li>Google Chrome</li>
<li>Firefox</li>
<li>Internet Explorer</li>
<li>Outlook</li>
<li>Thunderbird</li>
<li>Foxmail</li>
</ul>
</li>
</ul>
<div id="attachment_26538" style="width: 1653px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/19-min-10.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26538" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26538 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201643%20353'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="execution sequence - raccoon stealer" width="1643" height="353" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/19-min-10.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26538" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26538 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/19-min-10.png" alt="execution sequence - raccoon stealer" width="1643" height="353"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26538" class="wp-caption-text">6685955C5F006C2D83A92952EB5EB3FB9598C783 execution sequence</p></div>
<p>When running, Raccoon makes the following network requests:</p>
<div id="attachment_26539" style="width: 1296px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/20-min-9.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26539" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26539 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201286%20685'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="network requests raccoon stealer" width="1286" height="685" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/20-min-9.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26539" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26539 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/20-min-9.png" alt="network requests raccoon stealer" width="1286" height="685"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26539" class="wp-caption-text">3657CF5F2142C7E30F72E231E87518B82710DC1C network requests</p></div>
<p>Among these network requests, there is a connection to the <strong>blintick</strong> Telegram channel. Telegram was used by Raccoon&#8217;s creators to bypass blocking of the C&amp;C servers. To this end, the stealer makes a request to the Telegram channel and receives the encrypted address of the new C&amp;C server from the description. The first samples using this technique began appearing on VirusTotal in late May 2020.</p>
<div id="attachment_26540" style="width: 1640px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/21-min-8.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26540" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26540 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201630%20606'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Messages from the designers of the Raccoon stealer" width="1630" height="606" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/21-min-8.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26540" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26540 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/21-min-8.png" alt="Messages from the designers of the Raccoon stealer" width="1630" height="606"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26540" class="wp-caption-text">Messages from the designers of the Raccoon stealer</p></div>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="accordionList288"><div class="accordion-item"><div class="accordion-item__title"><div class="title-h5">Translation</div><div class="accordion-item__icon"><span class="material-icons">arrow_drop_down</span></div></div><div class="accordion-item__text"><p>The gate system has been updated. We have completely changed the traditional scheme. Detection decreases, keepalives increase. Build has been updated. The screenshot format has been changed to jpeg. Thanks for your feedback and support!</p>
</div></div>
                      <script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
                        jQuery(window).on('load', function () {

                          if (accordion288) {
                            let items = accordion288.querySelectorAll('.accordion-item');
                            let title = accordion288.querySelectorAll('.accordion-item__title');

                            function toggleAccordion() {
                              let thisItem = this.parentNode;
                              items.forEach((item) => {
                                if (thisItem == item) {
                                  thisItem.classList.toggle('active');
                                  return;
                                }
                                item.classList.remove('active');
                              });
                            }

                            title.forEach((question) => question.addEventListener('click', toggleAccordion));
                          } 
                        });
                      </script>
                      </div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><div id="attachment_26541" style="width: 397px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/22-min-7.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26541" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26541 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20387%20589'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="blintick Telegram channel and its description" width="387" height="589" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/22-min-7.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26541" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26541 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/22-min-7.png" alt="blintick Telegram channel and its description" width="387" height="589"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26541" class="wp-caption-text">blintick Telegram channel and its description</p></div>
<p><strong>Although the Raccoon stealer is distributed according to the MaaS model, all files distributed during the second wave accessed the same Telegram channel.</strong> This suggests that documents with malicious macros downloading Raccoon were distributed by the same group.</p>
<h2>Third wave</h2>
<p>The third wave of domain registration began on June 29, 2020:</p>
<ul>
<li>microsoft-cloud1[.]co.za</li>
<li>microsoft-cloud6[.]co.za</li>
<li>microsoft-cloud7[.]co.za</li>
<li>microsoft-cloud8[.]co.za</li>
<li>microsoft-cloud9[.]co.za</li>
<li>microsoft-cloud10[.]co.za</li>
<li>microsoft-cloud11[.]co.za</li>
<li>microsoft-cloud12[.]co.za</li>
<li>microsoft-cloud13[.]co.za</li>
<li>microsoft-cloud14[.]co.za</li>
<li>microsoft-cloud15[.]co.za</li>
</ul>
<p>All registered domains pointed to the IP address <em>102.130.112[.]195</em>. The first malicious files associated with this wave began to appear in public sandboxes as early as July 2, 2020. The names of these decoys are almost the same as the names of the files sent in the past: <em>BankStatement0109_13169.xlsm</em>, <em>My_Statement_4211.xlsm</em>, and so on. There are about 30 files associated with the domains and <em>cloud-server-updater1[.]co.za</em>.</p>
<div id="attachment_26542" style="width: 1821px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/23-min-7.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26542" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26542 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201811%20943'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="reccoon stealer Network infrastructure" width="1811" height="943" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/23-min-7.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26542" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26542 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/23-min-7.png" alt="reccoon stealer Network infrastructure" width="1811" height="943"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26542" class="wp-caption-text">Network infrastructure. File connections with domains involved in two waves established with the help of Group-IB Graph Network Analysis</p></div>
<p>The lure documents used as part of this wave look identical to the previous ones. Judging by their behavior after macros are activated, they were created by the same builder. Such builders make it possible to create office documents with malicious macros based on templates, which helps attackers distribute malicious files much faster and more efficiently.</p>
<div id="attachment_26543" style="width: 859px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/24-min-7.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26543" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26543 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20849%20348'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="raccoon stealer lure document sample" width="849" height="348" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/24-min-7.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26543" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26543 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/24-min-7.png" alt="raccoon stealer lure document sample" width="849" height="348"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26543" class="wp-caption-text"><br />618C894C06633E3D7ADD228531F6E775A180A7F7 lure document sample</p></div>
<p>Upon activating macros, the file &#8220;<em>My_Statement_1953.xlsm</em>&#8221; (SHA1: 618C894C06633E3D7ADD228531F6E775A180A7F7) sends a request to download the stealer file <em>http://microsoft-cloud13[.]co.za/msofficeupdate.exe</em>. The Raccoon stealer file (SHA1: 6639081791A8909F042E4A4197DF7051382B04E5) makes a series of requests to its C&amp;C server (<em>35.198.88[.]195</em>) and tries to download the file <em>http://cloud-server-updater1[.]co.za/doc/officeupdate.exe</em>, but receives an &#8220;<em>error 302</em>&#8221; and is redirected to <em>http://cloud-server-updater1[.]co.za/cgi-sys/suspendedpage.cgi</em> because the original domain is blocked. It seems that the sample was trying to download RAT AveMaria as before. In addition, all files related to this campaign made various network requests, including those to the Telegram channel <em>https://telete.in/blintick.</em></p>
<div id="attachment_26544" style="width: 712px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/25-min-5.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26544" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26544 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20702%20906'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Raccoon stealer network communication" width="702" height="906" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/25-min-5.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26544" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26544 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/25-min-5.png" alt="Raccoon stealer network communication" width="702" height="906"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26544" class="wp-caption-text">6639081791A8909F042E4A4197DF7051382B04E5 Raccoon stealer network communication</p></div>
<h2>Using loaders</h2>
<p><strong>During this campaign, the attackers also experimented with various loaders. While analyzing the infrastructure, we discovered the Buer and Smoke loaders.</strong></p>
<p>On April 30, 2020, an xls document (SHA1: 6c6680659b09d18ccab0f933daf5bf1910168b1a) was uploaded to VirusTotal. When the malicious code is executed, it downloads the payload from <em>http://cloud-server-updater2.co[.]za/doc/buer.exe</em>.</p>
<div id="attachment_26545" style="width: 1842px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/26-min-6.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26545" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26545 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201832%20817'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="racoon stealer network communication" width="1832" height="817" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/26-min-6.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26545" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26545 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/26-min-6.png" alt="racoon stealer network communication" width="1832" height="817"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26545" class="wp-caption-text">SHA1:6c6680659b09d18ccab0f933daf5bf1910168b1a file network communication established with the help of Group-IB Graph Network Analysis</p></div>
<p>Apart from that, the files were uploaded to a public resource: bazaar.abuse[.]ch.</p>
<p>The file names and the tags attached refer to the <a href="https://bazaar.abuse.ch/sample/bc96c38e3f85c43923a37f57c096eb5b3913b516dbcb11b46cb9cfd0c1d167ce/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener">Buer loader</a>.</p>
<div id="attachment_26546" style="width: 963px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/27-min-5.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26546" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26546 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20953%20812'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="raccoon stealer network communication" width="953" height="812" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/27-min-5.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26546" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26546 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/27-min-5.png" alt="raccoon stealer network communication" width="953" height="812"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26546" class="wp-caption-text">SHA1:<a href="https://bazaar.abuse.ch/sample/522a42c27449aba54fa539f28082f1ddc2e8e6ea4f3dd29141f702a5ad82e29f/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener">7b1a5d9bb21d852a6dbf3146fabb1cd1ca276ed9</a> file network communication</p></div>
<p>While monitoring the adversary infrastructure, we identified a batch of domains registered by the attackers between August 24 and September 12, 2020. Examples of such domains are presented below:</p>
</div>

</div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><div id="attachment_26547" style="width: 1622px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/28-min-5.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26547" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26547 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201612%20549'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Similar WHOIS domain records" width="1612" height="549" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/28-min-5.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26547" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26547 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/28-min-5.png" alt="Similar WHOIS domain records" width="1612" height="549"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26547" class="wp-caption-text">Similar WHOIS domain records</p></div>
<p>The WHOIS records for these domains match the WHOIS records for those discovered previously in this campaign. On August 26, 2020, malicious files related to the domains code-cloud[1-6][.]co.za and <em>google-document[.]co.za</em> began appearing on public resources. One of these files is &#8220;<em>BankStatement_1390868739.doc</em>&#8221; (SHA1: ed5c20371bae393df0a713be72220b055e5cbdad).</p>
<div id="attachment_26548" style="width: 1662px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/29-min-5.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26548" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26548 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201652%20824'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="network communication raccoon stealer" width="1652" height="824" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/29-min-5.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26548" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26548 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/29-min-5.png" alt="network communication raccoon stealer" width="1652" height="824"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26548" class="wp-caption-text">SHA1: ed5c20371bae393df0a713be72220b055e5cbdad file network communication established with the help of Group-IB Graph Network Analysis</p></div>
<p>When the malicious code is executed, the file <a href="https://urlhaus.abuse.ch/url/441030/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener">downloads</a> the payload from <em>http://google-document[.]co.za/doc/loader.exe</em>. Signature analysis showed that the downloaded file is a Smoke loader sample.</p>
<div id="attachment_26549" style="width: 951px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/30-min-4.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26549" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26549 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20941%20594'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt=""loader.exe" file analysis and Smoke loader tag" width="941" height="594" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/30-min-4.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26549" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26549 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/30-min-4.png" alt=""loader.exe" file analysis and Smoke loader tag" width="941" height="594"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26549" class="wp-caption-text">&#8220;loader.exe&#8221; file analysis and Smoke loader tag</p></div>
<p><strong>The fact that the cybercriminals additionally use loaders in their campaigns could indicate that they are still searching for the most effective tools.</strong></p>
<h2>Fourth wave</h2>
<p>Some of the domains registered in early September 2020 mimicked Adobe in their names. From September 14, 2020, Group-IB experts found Mephistophilus with an identical pattern on these hosts, just like during the first wave.</p>
<div id="attachment_26550" style="width: 1853px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/31-min-4.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26550" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26550 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201843%20931'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Connection between the Mephistophilus infrastructure and the 2019 and 2020 campaigns" width="1843" height="931" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/31-min-4.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26550" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26550 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/31-min-4.png" alt="Connection between the Mephistophilus infrastructure and the 2019 and 2020 campaigns" width="1843" height="931"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26550" class="wp-caption-text">Connection between the Mephistophilus infrastructure and the 2019 and 2020 campaigns established with the help of Group-IB Graph Network Analysis</p></div>
<div id="attachment_26551" style="width: 1610px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/32-min-4.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26551" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26551 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201600%201200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Screenshot of a Mephistophilus decoy page" width="1600" height="1200" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/32-min-4.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26551" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26551 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/32-min-4.png" alt="Screenshot of a Mephistophilus decoy page" width="1600" height="1200"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26551" class="wp-caption-text">Screenshot of a Mephistophilus decoy page</p></div>
<p>Clicking on the &#8220;Download&#8221; plugin button downloads the Raccoon stealer file SHA1: bcfb45e5451435530156f1f02ddbb9cadf6338e9 from <em>https://updateforadobenew[.]co.za/file_d/adobe-reader-v13.11.1.3.exe</em>.</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="gallery swiper gallery-swiper-857"><div class="swiper-wrapper"><div class="swiper-slide gallery__item wp-caption"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/33-min-4.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="raccoon stealer file in group-ib managed xdr interface" title="33-min" class="webpexpress-processed" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/33-min-4.png"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/33-min-4.png" alt="raccoon stealer file in group-ib managed xdr interface" title="33-min" class="webpexpress-processed"></noscript></picture><p class="small wp-caption-text">Data from Group-IB Managed XDR Polygon</p></div><div class="swiper-slide gallery__item wp-caption"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/34-min-2.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%200%200'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="MITRE ATT&CK matrix of the raccoon stealer file analyzed" title="34-min" class="webpexpress-processed" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/34-min-2.png"><noscript><img src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/34-min-2.png" alt="MITRE ATT&CK matrix of the raccoon stealer file analyzed" title="34-min" class="webpexpress-processed"></noscript></picture><p class="small wp-caption-text">MITRE ATT&CK matrix of the file analyzed</p></div></div><div class="swiper-pagination"></div><div class="swiper-button-next"></div><div class="swiper-button-prev"></div></div></div>

                  <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>Note: Around mid-July 2020, the attackers deleted their Telegram channel. It was restored on September 14, 2020 and the description contained the encrypted address of the active C&amp;C server. At the time of writing, the channel is inactive again.</p>
<div id="attachment_26554" style="width: 778px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/35-min-3.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26554" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26554 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20768%20481'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="blintick Telegram channel content" width="768" height="481" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/35-min-3.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26554" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26554 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/35-min-3.png" alt="blintick Telegram channel content" width="768" height="481"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26554" class="wp-caption-text">blintick Telegram channel content</p></div>
<h2>Relation to FakeSecurity</h2>
<p><strong>This malicious campaign bears a striking resemblance to a series of FakeSecurity JS-sniffer attacks</strong> described by Group-IB in November 2019. Past attacks targeted owners of online stores powered by Magento CMS. In the campaign <a href="/blog/fakesecurity/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">described</a> previously, the attackers also used such tools as the Vidar stealer and the Mephistophilus phishing kit, with an identical template for Adobe updates. In addition, the attackers used the same hosting service to register domains in both campaigns.</p>
<p><strong>In the 2020 campaign, the same attack vector was used and involved subsequent distribution of the Raccoon stealer.</strong> In addition, the investigation revealed messages sent to several online stores from <a href="/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection" class="__cf_email__" data-cfemail="e486819e878bca95918d9781d5dddcdca49394ca9488">[email&#160;protected]</a> and <a href="/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection" class="__cf_email__" data-cfemail="f59a8081819c94db99909b90c4cccdc0b58285db8599">[email&#160;protected]</a>.</p>
<p>A detailed analysis of the first-wave malware distribution via Mephistophilus phishing pages revealed a link between the domains involved in this campaign (in particular documents-cloud-server*[.]co.za) and the FakeSecurity campaign. During the 2020 campaign, phishing pages were available at the following URLs:</p>
<div id="attachment_26555" style="width: 1451px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/36-min-4.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26555" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26555 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201441%20503'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="List of domains with an identical structure" width="1441" height="503" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/36-min-4.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26555" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26555 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/36-min-4.png" alt="List of domains with an identical structure" width="1441" height="503"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26555" class="wp-caption-text">List of domains with an identical structure</p></div>
<p>According to urlscan[.]io, more than 20 sites with a similar structure were discovered, but the one that stands out is alloaypparel[.]com. It was used in the FakeSecurity campaign.</p>
<p>Since March 2020, Group-IB specialists have started detecting online store infections with a JS sniffer obfuscated by the aaencode algorithm (https://utf-8.jp/public/aaencode.html). The malware was loaded from get-js[.]com. WHOIS records similar to those used previously by this group were located at get-js[.]com:</p>
<ul>
<li>fiswedbesign[.]com</li>
<li>alloaypparel[.]com</li>
<li>firstofbanks[.]com</li>
<li>magento-security[.]org</li>
<li>mage-security[.]org</li>
</ul>
<div id="attachment_26556" style="width: 1930px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/37-min-3.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26556" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26556 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201920%201080'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Connection between FakeSecutiry infrastructure during the 2019 campaign and raccoon stealer domain" width="1920" height="1080" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/37-min-3.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26556" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26556 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/37-min-3.png" alt="Connection between FakeSecutiry infrastructure during the 2019 campaign and raccoon stealer domain" width="1920" height="1080"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26556" class="wp-caption-text">Connection between FakeSecutiry infrastructure during the 2019 campaign and the domain get-js[.]com built with the help of Group-IB Graph Network Analysis</p></div>
<div id="attachment_26557" style="width: 1430px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/38-min-3.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26557" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26557 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201420%20758'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Part of JS-sniffer code obfuscated with aaencode" width="1420" height="758" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/38-min-3.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26557" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26557 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/38-min-3.png" alt="Part of JS-sniffer code obfuscated with aaencode" width="1420" height="758"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26557" class="wp-caption-text">Part of JS-sniffer code obfuscated with aaencode</p></div>
<p>After deobfuscating it, <strong>Group-IB established that the malware used for infections was a modified version of the FakeSecurity JS-sniffer.</strong> Its distribution was analyzed in November 2019.</p>
<div id="attachment_26558" style="width: 1031px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/39-min-3.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26558" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26558 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201021%20831'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Deobfuscated code of the FakeSecurity JS-sniffer modified version" width="1021" height="831" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/39-min-3.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26558" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26558 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/39-min-3.png" alt="Deobfuscated code of the FakeSecurity JS-sniffer modified version" width="1021" height="831"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26558" class="wp-caption-text">Deobfuscated code of the FakeSecurity JS-sniffer modified version</p></div>
<p>In May 2020, Group-IB discovered new infected online stores. Once again, the attackers used a modified FakeSecurity JS-sniffer obfuscated with aaencode. The malware was injected either by a link using a script tag or by modifying existing JavaScript files on the site. The JS-sniffer was used to compromise over 20 online stores between May and September 2020. The following domains were used to store the code and collect stolen bank card data during the new campaign:</p>
<ul>
<li>cloud-js[.]co.za</li>
<li>host-js[.]co.za</li>
<li>magento-cloud[.]co.za</li>
<li>magento-js[.]co.za</li>
<li>magento-security[.]co.za</li>
<li>marketplace-magento[.]co.za</li>
<li>marketplacemagento[.]co.za</li>
<li>node-js[.]co.za</li>
<li>node-js[.]co.za</li>
<li>payment-js[.]co.za</li>
<li>security-js[.]co.za</li>
<li>web-js[.]co.za</li>
</ul>
<p>Created on April 24, 2020 (during the second wave), these domains were registered with the same registrars as those used to distribute the Vidar and Raccoon stealers and the Buer and Smoke loaders.</p>
<p><strong>The format of the links to the JS-sniffer files combined with the malware family type suggest that FakeSecurity JS-sniffer operators are behind the campaign to infect online stores.</strong></p>
<p>In addition, some domains involved in the campaign under investigation hosted a parked page labeled &#8220;test page&#8221;, like the one hosted on FakeSecurity domains:</p>
<ul>
<li>https://urlscan.io/result/0299b3e5-cbba-40be-adce-7ba437e4cb39/ microsoft-cloud10[.]co.za</li>
<li>https://urlscan.io/result/8f244d1b-2186-4db5-9c52-6122584dafa9/ &#8211; documents-cloud-server[.]co.za</li>
</ul>
</div>

                  <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p><strong>The evidence found indicates that the operators of the FakeSecurity JS-sniffer family are likely to be behind the multi-stage malicious campaign described above.</strong> According to our information, even though the group gains initial access using non-self-developed tools sold or rented on darknet forums, it continues to operate its exclusive JS-sniffer.</p>
<h2>Recommendations</h2>
<p>Below you can see attackers&#8217; TTPs and relevant mitigation and defense techniques in accordance with MITRE ATT&amp;CK and MITRE Shield that we recommend to use to protect against and prevent cyberattacks.</p>
<p>All the mitigation and defense techniques are implemented in Group-IB&#8217;s products intended for the protection against cyberattacks at early stages. If you have any questions or suspect that you&#8217;re being attacked email us at <a href="/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#3e4c5b4d4e51504d5b7e5d5b4c4a1359575c105d5153"><span class="__cf_email__" data-cfemail="6b190e181b0405180e2b080e191f460c020945080406">[email&#160;protected]</span></a>.</p>

                        jQuery(window).on('load', function () {
                          let accordion751 = document.querySelector('.accordionList751');
                          if (accordion751) {
                            let items = accordion751.querySelectorAll('.accordion-item');
                            let title = accordion751.querySelectorAll('.accordion-item__title');

                            function toggleAccordion() {
                              let thisItem = this.parentNode;
                              items.forEach((item) => {
                                if (thisItem == item) {
                                  thisItem.classList.toggle('active');
                                  return;
                                }
                                item.classList.remove('active');
                              });
                            }

                            title.forEach((question) => question.addEventListener('click', toggleAccordion));
                          } 
                        });
                      </script>

    var $root = jQuery('html, body');


    jQuery('a[href^="#"].scrollto').click(function() {
        var href = jQuery.attr(this, 'href');
        var headerHeight = 100;

        $root.animate({
                scrollTop: jQuery(href).offset().top - headerHeight
            },
            300,
        );

        return false;
    });

    // SMOOTH SCROLL WITH NO HASH
    var $root = jQuery('html, body');


    jQuery('a[href^="#"].scrolltonohash').click(function() {
        var href = jQuery.attr(this, 'href');
        var headerHeight = 100;

        $root.animate({
                scrollTop: jQuery(href).offset().top - headerHeight
            },
            300
        );

        return false;
    });
</script>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    function readingTime() {
        const text = document.getElementById("blog-post-content").innerText;
        const wpm = 225;
        const words = text.trim().split(/\s+/).length;
        const time = Math.ceil(words / wpm);
        document.getElementById("reading-time").innerText = time;
    }
    readingTime();
</script>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    document.getElementById("share-url").value = window.location.href;
    document.getElementById("share-url-mobile").value = window.location.href;
    jQuery('.share-url__copy').click(function() {
        jQuery('.share-url__copy').addClass('success');
        navigator.clipboard.writeText(window.location.href);
        setTimeout(() => {
            jQuery('.share-url__copy').removeClass('success');
        }, 1500);
    });
</script>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    var carousel123 = new Swiper('.carousel123', {
        grabCursor: true,
        pagination: {
            el: '.swiper-pagination',
            clickable: true,
        },
    });

    var authorsMoreThan2Swiper = new Swiper('.authors-more-than-2-swiper', {
        grabCursor: true,
        pagination: {
            el: '.swiper-pagination',
            clickable: true,
        },
        breakpoints: {
            0: {
                slidesPerView: 'auto',
                spaceBetween: 16,
                slidesPerView: 1,
                slidesPerGroup: 1,
            },
            620: {
                spaceBetween: 24,
                slidesPerView: 2,
                slidesPerGroup: 2,
            },
        },
    });

    function positioningSidebar() {
        let sidebar = document.getElementById('sidebar');
        let sidebarHeight = sidebar.offsetHeight;
        let windowHeight = document.documentElement.clientHeight;
        if (windowHeight < (sidebarHeight + 48)) {
            let sidebarAuthors = sidebar.querySelector('.sidebar__authors');
            let sidebarMorePosts = sidebar.querySelector('.sidebar__more-posts');
            let authorsHeight = sidebarAuthors ? sidebarAuthors.offsetHeight : 0;
            let morePostsHeight = sidebarMorePosts ? sidebarMorePosts.offsetHeight : 0;
            let sidebarTop = authorsHeight + morePostsHeight + 140;
            jQuery(sidebar).css('top', -sidebarTop + 'px');
        } else {
            jQuery(sidebar).css('top', '72px');
        }
    }
    window.addEventListener('resize', positioningSidebar);
    positioningSidebar();

    if (localStorage.getItem('theme')) {
        if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'dark') {
            jQuery('body').addClass('theme--dark');
        } else if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'light') {
            jQuery('body').addClass('theme--white');
        }
    } else {
        localStorage.setItem('theme', 'dark');
        jQuery('body').addClass('theme--dark');
    }

    jQuery('.theme-toggle').click(function() {
        if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'dark') {
            localStorage.setItem('theme', 'light');
        } else {
            localStorage.setItem('theme', 'dark');
        }
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('no-transition');
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('theme--dark');
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('theme--white');
        setTimeout(() => {
            jQuery('body').toggleClass('no-transition');
        }, 250);
    });
    jQuery('.share-toggle').click(function() {
        jQuery('.share-toggle').toggleClass('active');
        jQuery('.share-widget').toggleClass('show');
    });
</script>
